weeks after the official start of the Division I season when practice began, we were treated to wrestle-offs from three of the nation&s;s best programs, as well as our first glimpse of the newly minted Little Rock squad.&l;/p&g;&q;,&q;content&q;:&q;&l;p&g;Two weeks after the &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/articles/6572752-recapping-the-first-day-of-ncaa-wrestling-practice-for-the-2019-20-season\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;official start&l;/a&g; of the Division I season when practice began, we were treated to wrestle-offs from three of the nation&a;#39;s best programs, as well as our first glimpse of the newly minted Little Rock squad.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;The wrestle-offs for #3 Ohio State provided an initial glimpse of the Covelli Center to the Buckeye faithful. Ninth-ranked Lehigh held their annual Brown &a;amp; White wrestle-offs in the &a;quot;Snake Pit,&a;quot; and #20 North Carolina showed off the latest bunch of Tar Heels in their return to Carmichael Arena. Also doing wrestle-offs tonight were the North Dakota State Bison.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Below are complete results from each team&a;#39;s wrestle-offs tonight.&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;#3 Ohio State&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;125:&l;/strong&g; Malik Heinselman 7-2 over Hunter Lucas&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;133:&l;/strong&g; Jordan Decatur 13-4 over Dylan Koontz&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;141:&l;/strong&g; Luke Pletcher 7-3 over Quinn Kinner&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;149:&l;/strong&g; Sammy Sasso 20-7 over Trey Grenier&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;157:&l;/strong&g; Elijah Cleary 4-2 (TB1) over Jaden Mattox&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;165:&l;/strong&g; Carson Kharchla 3-2 over Ethan Smith&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;174:&l;/strong&g; Rocky Jordan 2-1 (TB1) over Kaleb Romero&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;184:&l;/strong&g; Zach Steiner 5-4 over Gavin Hoffman&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;197:&l;/strong&g; Kollin Moore 19-4 over Tyler Stein&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;True freshman Carson Kharchla took out #12 Ethan Smith at 165, and his fellow true freshman Jordan Decatur won his wrestle-off as well. At 184, Zach Steiner upset #17 Gavin Hoffman. At 157, #7 Josh Humphreys rolled to a 9-0 shutout and #20 Chris Weiler teched Eli Grape at 184. Published&q;,&q;status_color&q;:&q;#CDEB8B&q;,&q;premium&q;:true,&q;node&q;:{&q;categories&q;:[{&q;id&q;:6,&q;name&q;:&q;Preview&q;,&q;display_name&q;:null,&q;parent&q;:{&q;id&q;:1,&q;name&q;:&q;Content Type&q;,&q;display_name&q;:null,&q;parent&q;:null,&q;created_at&q;:&q;2018-01-22T21:12:47+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:&q;2019-08-20T20:27:55+00:00&q;},&q;created_at&q;:&q;2018-01-22T21:14:10+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:null}],&q;primary_event_association&q;:null,&q;primary_event_or_series_association&q;:null,&q;people&q;:[],&q;teams&q;:[],&q;id&q;:6586184,&q;site&q;:{&q;domain&q;:&q;www.flowrestling.org&q;,&q;id&q;:2,&q;name&q;:&q;FloWrestling&q;,&q;code&q;:&q;flowrestling&q;,&q;host&q;:&q;http://www.flowrestling.org&q;,&q;active&q;:true,&q;type&q;:&q;site&q;,&q;version&q;:3,&q;color&q;:&q;#FF140F&q;,&q;hero_image&q;:&q;https://d6fm3yzmawlcs.cloudfront.net/mobileVerticalBackground/mobile_bg_flowrestling.jpg&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:null,&q;show_on_mobile&q;:true,&q;sport_name&q;:&q;Wrestling&q;},&q;view_count&q;:8087,&q;legacy_id&q;:null,&q;search_indexed_at&q;:&q;2019-10-24T21:50:22+00:00&q;,&q;created_at&q;:&q;2019-10-22T00:56:12+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:&q;2019-10-24T21:50:22+00:00&q;},&q;enable_interstitial_ad&q;:true,&q;enable_pre_roll_ads&q;:true,&q;shareable_link&q;:&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/articles/6586184-2019-20-ncaa-preview-predictions-149-pounds&q;,&q;created_at&q;:&q;2019-10-22T00:56:12+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:&q;2019-10-24T21:50:17+00:00&q;,&q;categories&q;:[{&q;id&q;:6,&q;name&q;:&q;Preview&q;,&q;display_name&q;:null,&q;parent&q;:{&q;id&q;:1,&q;name&q;:&q;Content Type&q;,&q;display_name&q;:null,&q;parent&q;:null,&q;created_at&q;:&q;2018-01-22T21:12:47+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:&q;2019-08-20T20:27:55+00:00&q;},&q;created_at&q;:&q;2018-01-22T21:14:10+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:null}],&q;mobile_link&q;:&q;https://admin.flowrestling.org/mobile/articles/6586184-2019-20-ncaa-preview-predictions-149-pounds&q;,&q;preview_text&q;:&q;&l;p&g;Summer has turned to fall, which means the leaves (and Flo’s logo) are changing from green to red. It also means folkstyle has returned, with the start of the NCAA season less than two weeks away.&l;/p&g;&q;,&q;content&q;:&q;&l;p&g;Summer has turned to fall, which means the leaves (and Flo&a;rsquo;s logo) are changing from green to red. It also means folkstyle has returned, with the start of the NCAA season less than two weeks away.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Preview szn will take into account many things, from &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/rankings/6567575-2019-20-ncaa-di-rankings/32627-149-austin-oconnor\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;weight class rankings&l;/a&g; to &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/articles/6548795-2020-redshirt-report-149-pounds\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;redshirt reports&l;/a&g; to the &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/rankings/6568197-2020-crystal-ball-rankings-ncaa-di-by-willie-saylor/32696-super-young-149\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;crystal ball rankings&l;/a&g;, to try to give you as clear a picture as possible of what&a;rsquo;s in store for the 2019-20 season. The specter of Olympic Trials will hang over the year, impacting weight class changes and the depth of some brackets at the NCAA tournament in Minneapolis.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Over a third of the guys in the final &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/rankings/6249786-ncaa-rankings-2018-19/31207-149-mob-rule\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;2018-19 ranking&l;/a&g; have graduated from 149, including the top three placers from Pittsburgh. Not to mention #5 is redshirting, while #10 and #16 are moving up in weight. That leaves it as perhaps the most wide-open weight for now, along with some of the most interesting additions to the field from incoming freshmen and guys changing weights.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;NCAA Previews:&l;/strong&g; &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/articles/6578344-2019-20-ncaa-preview-predictions-125-pounds\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;125&l;/a&g; | &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/articles/6581952-2019-20-ncaa-preview-predictions-133-pounds\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;133&l;/a&g; | &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/articles/6585983-2019-20-ncaa-preview-predictions-141-pounds\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;141&l;/a&g;&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;The Favorite: To Be Determined&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;A throne long held by Zain Retherford, and then taken by Anthony Ashnault over the likes of Micah Jordan and Matt Kolodzik at the end of last year, 149 is now more up for grabs than it&a;rsquo;s been in a long time. So this portion will remain empty, and our contenders list will be expanded.&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;The Contenders&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#1 Austin O&a;rsquo;Connor, North Carolina&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#2 Brock Mauller, Missouri&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#3 Jarrett Degen, Iowa State&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#5 Sammy Sasso, Ohio State&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#11 Brayton Lee, Minnesota&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;The first preseason #1 of the Coleman Scott era, now the pressure&a;rsquo;s on O&a;rsquo;Connor to deliver a national title to Chapel Hill, which would be just the fourth in UNC history. Going back through the photos of NCAAs it&a;rsquo;s quite apparent the emotion he had on his face after making the semis and guaranteeing himself an All-American placement as a freshman. The 12th overall recruit on the &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/rankings/6031476-class-of-2017-big-board/16233-final-class-of-2017-big-board\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;2017 Big Board&l;/a&g;, O&a;rsquo;Connor has been a winner his whole career. But will a title run for the St. Rita product look similar to the one Jason Tsirtsis had, or can he increase his offensive output from the 3.75 points per match he averaged in the postseason?&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Our preseason #2 in Mauller suffered a 4-3 defeat in the consi semis at the hands of O&a;rsquo;Connor spelled the difference between their third and sixth-place finishes at NCAAs. The champ right before Zain at 149 was another Missouri wrestler in Drake Houdashelt, so Brian Smith may want to call upon his former Tiger for inspiration. After taking over for an injured Grant Leeth last season, there is no confusion this year, with Jaydin Eierman redshirting and Leeth moving down to 141.&l;/p&g;&l;div class=\&q;froala-video fr-draggable\&q;&g;&l;span class=\&q;fr-video fr-fvc fr-dvi fr-draggable\&q; contenteditable=\&q;false\&q;&g;&l;iframe width=\&q;640\&q; height=\&q;360\&q; class=\&q;flo-video-embed embed embed-responsive-item fr-draggable\&q; src=\&q;/embed/b97gglA9W\&q;&g;&l;/iframe&g;&l;/span&g;&l;/div&g;&l;p&g;&l;br&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Mauller and #3 Jarrett Degen split their four matches last year, so there&a;rsquo;s really not much differentiating those two. Degen went from the Cyclones&a;#39; only NCAA qualifier to their only returning placer and perhaps one of the most important wrestlers in the program looking towards the 2020 national tournament in Minnesota. He&a;rsquo;s going to be battle-tested coming through the &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/rankings/6534554-2019-20-big-12-conference-rankings/32117-149-big-12-rankings\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;Big 12&l;/a&g;, as this might be the best weight in the conference.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Two guys who squared off for the Junior world team spot round out our list of contenders. Sammy Sasso came in to high school and impacted Pennsylvania AAA wrestling right away at Nazareth, became the face of District XI, had a widely followed recruitment, and wound up at the only school to win team trophies each of the past five years. His third-place showing at Midlands included wins over Pat Lugo, Alec Pantaleo, and Josh Maruca. He finished off his year with a &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/video/6341985-149-lbs-final-brayton-lee-minnesota-vs-sammy-sasso-ohio-state\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;6-4 victory&l;/a&g; over Brayton Lee in the finals of the Edinboro Open.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Lee was a three-time Indiana state champ out of the same club that produced Chad Red. For nearly his entire high school career, he placed at all the majors &a;mdash; Akron, Super 32, Fargo &a;mdash; beating just about all his peers, but never finishing at the top of the heap. Then he finally won Fargo as a graduated senior, and rebounded from getting teched by Sasso in the Open finals to beat him twice en route to the Junior world team spot. He&a;rsquo;s always been a shade better in freestyle due to his neutral prowess, but his pace is just as difficult to deal with in folkstyle.&l;/p&g;&l;div class=\&q;froala-video fr-draggable\&q;&g;&l;span class=\&q;fr-video fr-fvc fr-dvi fr-draggable\&q; contenteditable=\&q;false\&q;&g;&l;iframe width=\&q;640\&q; height=\&q;360\&q; class=\&q;flo-video-embed embed embed-responsive-item fr-draggable\&q; src=\&q;/embed/gYqPlEva8\&q;&g;&l;/iframe&g;&l;/span&g;&l;/div&g;&l;p&g;&l;br&g;&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;Best of the Rest&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#4 Brock Zacherl, Clarion&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#6 Pat Lugo, Iowa&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#7 Boo Lewallen, Oklahoma State&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#8 Max Thomsen, Northern Iowa&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#10 Mike D&a;rsquo;Angelo, Princeton&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;By sheer happenstance I wound up calling the last match of Zacherl&a;rsquo;s season. It was at CKLV, and seemed like a huge blow to Clarion. But thanks to the NCAA&a;rsquo;s much-improved positions on medical redshirts and sixth years, Zacherl gets one more crack at a national title in perhaps an even better position this time around. A three-time qualifier, he has just 12 career losses.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Lugo is the type of guy Iowa will be depending on to make a jump this year as they push for a title. But he&a;rsquo;s ranked sixth to enter the season, and was eighth last year along with a Round of 12 finish as a freshman, so the data thus far in his career suggests his preseason ranking is about what his talent level is, placement-wise. Expect a lot of overtime, as over 20 percent of his matches last season went beyond seven minutes.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Boo Lewallen committed to Oklahoma State after his junior year of high school, but his career is nearing its close. The issue for Lewallen has never been desire or skill or practice partners &a;mdash; it&a;rsquo;s been health and opportunity. His one and only full season he was an All-American, beating four other AAs that year, but he&a;#39;s constantly battling injuries. The first four weights will be crucial for the Cowboys, especially with Kaid Brock and Daton Fix out. We &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/video/6371217-a-day-in-stillwater-with-boo-lewallen\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;spent the day&l;/a&g; with him back in February before the Iowa dual.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Max Thomsen placed fifth as a freshman and has lost in the bloodround twice since then. A key piece in the Panthers&a;#39; strong &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/rankings/6031440-2015-recruiting-class-rankings/14107-2015-recruiting-class-rankings\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;2015 recruiting class&l;/a&g;, it is their last shot to put it all together as a group and give UNI their best NCAA tournament finish of the Doug Schwab era. He&a;#39;s going to see Degen and Lewallen multiple times in the second half of the year, the first of which he is 3-0 against and the second he is 1-2 against, losing the last two.&l;/p&g;&l;div class=\&q;froala-video fr-draggable\&q;&g;&l;span class=\&q;fr-video fr-fvc fr-dvi fr-draggable\&q; contenteditable=\&q;false\&q;&g;&l;iframe width=\&q;640\&q; height=\&q;360\&q; class=\&q;flo-video-embed embed embed-responsive-item fr-draggable\&q; src=\&q;/embed/MTk3MTAzNzgyMA==\&q;&g;&l;/iframe&g;&l;/span&g;&l;/div&g;&l;p&g;&l;br&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;With Matt Kolodzik focusing on freestyle, Mike D&a;#39;Angelo slides down to 149 in his final year for the Tigers. A two-time national qualifier up at 157, he went 19-4 while taking a greyshirt. An NHSCA senior champ and Super 32 finalist from New York, D&a;#39;Angelo has placed three times before at EIWAs but never claimed a conference title.&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;New Blood&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#14 Griffin Parriott, Purdue&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#21 Yahya Thomas, Northwestern&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Andrew Alirez, Northern Colorado&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Kevon Davenport, Nebraska&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Matt Grippi, NC State&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;We&a;#39;ve already covered Lee and Sasso above in the contenders section, so to make up for that we&a;#39;re adding some weight changes to this portion. Parriott was unquestionably a blue-chipper coming out, #7 on the 2016 Big Board, but didn&a;#39;t qualify for NCAAs as a freshman and was 2-2 there last year. But Boilermaker assistant AJ Schopp said Parriott is a new man this season, focused and ready to roll.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Meanwhile, Thomas battled weight issues down at 141, but then looked great in making the Junior world team at 65kg. He didn&a;#39;t even get a chance to qualify for the national tournament as he did not wrestle at Big Tens, but this should be his breakout year, especially with Ryan Deakin at the weight above him.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Perhaps the true freshman I&a;#39;m most excited to watch is Andrew Alirez. Partially because we&a;#39;ve known he&a;#39;s going right away, partially because most of our data on him is in freestyle, and partially because he competes for a smaller program trying to become one of the big dogs. But the main reason is that he&a;#39;s absolutely fearless and a pit bull. Yes, he&a;#39;ll win a ton of matches with his swing single. But he&a;#39;s exactly the kind of kid I believe won&a;#39;t fold in a bloodround match in March.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Willie put Kevon in the Round of 12 in his first batch of crystal ball rankings, but it still is yet to be determined if he redshirts or starts right away over #22 Collin Purinton, a fifth-year senior. Similarly, AJ Leitten was elected a team captain for the Wolfpack so we might not see Matt Grippi this season.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;Key Dates&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Nov. 24:&l;/strong&g; Minnesota at Oklahoma State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Nov. 24:&l;/strong&g; Iowa at Iowa State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Dec. 6-7:&l;/strong&g; Cliff Keen Las Vegas&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Dec. 29-30:&l;/strong&g; Midlands&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Jan. 1-2:&l;/strong&g; Southern Scuffle&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Jan. 12:&l;/strong&g; Missouri at Northern Iowa&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Jan. 12:&l;/strong&g; Northern Colorado at Oklahoma State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Jan. 17:&l;/strong&g; Northern Iowa at Northern Colorado&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Jan. 24:&l;/strong&g; Ohio State at Iowa&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Jan. 26:&l;/strong&g; Oklahoma State at Iowa State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Jan. 26:&l;/strong&g; Ohio State at Minnesota&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Feb. 2:&l;/strong&g; Missouri at Oklahoma State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Feb. 15:&l;/strong&g; Minnesota at Iowa&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Feb. 16:&l;/strong&g; Northern Iowa at Iowa State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Feb. 22:&l;/strong&g; Iowa State at Missouri&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Feb. 23:&l;/strong&g; Oklahoma State at Iowa&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Thanks to the composition of this weight, damn near every weekend there is going to be a great matchup. The weekend before Thanksgiving we&a;#39;ll get #11 Brayton Lee against #7 Boo Lewallen at Gallagher-Iba and another match between #6 Pat Lugo and #3 Jarrett Degen in Ames, just one of several juicy matchups when the Hawkeyes face the Cyclones.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Then at CKLV we could see the following guys: Degen, Lee, #4 Brock Zacherl, #5 Sammy Sasso, #8 Max Thomsen, and stud freshman Alirez, to go along with six other guys in the preseason top 20, as well as #21 Yahya Thomas. An excellent way to mark yourself as a front runner for the NCAA title a month into the season.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Then at Midlands we&a;#39;ll see O&a;#39;Connor&a;#39;s first test of the year, with Lugo and Thomsen in the mix, along with D&a;#39;Angelo, Parriott, and Yahya. Then a couple of days later at the Scuffle we&a;#39;ll get Degen, Lewallen, and Alirez before the Big 12 schedule even starts, plus #15 Requir van der Merwe and #18 Russell Rohlfing out of the Pac-12.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Sunday, January 12 will be fun: Brock Mauller against Max Thomsen and Alirez vs Lewallen. Turning right around that next week Alirez will face Thomsen, potentially with both of them coming off losses and hungry to get back on track. Did I mention the Big 12 is going to be fun this year?&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;We&a;#39;ve barely mentioned the Big Ten, but that&a;#39;ll start picking up in late January with Sasso against Lugo at Carver-Hawkeye, then Sasso vs Brayton in Minneapolis in one of the more brutal weekends of the year. That same weekend Boo faces Degen in Ames.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Heading into February, we get Mauller vs Lewallen, and then perhaps our only break of the season. We finish with a flurry, as Brayton takes on Lugo followed by Thomsen against Degen the next day. Closing out the year we&a;#39;ll get Degen vs Mauller and Lewallen facing Lugo.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Phew. Don&a;#39;t envy our rankers for 149.&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;Nomad&a;#39;s Predictions&l;/h2&g;&l;ol&g;&l;li&g;Sammy Sasso, Ohio State&l;/li&g;&l;li&g;Austin O&a;#39;Connor, North Carolina&l;/li&g;&l;li&g;Brock Mauller, Missouri&l;/li&g;&l;li&g;Brayton Lee, Minnesota&l;/li&g;&l;li&g;Jarrett Degen, Iowa State&l;/li&g;&l;li&g;Andrew Alirez, Northern Colorado&l;/li&g;&l;li&g;Max Thomsen, Northern Iowa&l;/li&g;&l;li&g;Pat Lugo, Iowa&l;/li&g;&l;/ol&g;&l;p&g;Just like last year, no matter who I pick, it&a;#39;s someone who&a;#39;s never done it before. Given the number of important matches listed above, it&a;#39;s also highly unlikely we see someone go undefeated. So I&a;#39;m picking the guy I think is most likely to put together five matches in March: Sammy Sasso.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Yung Sasso has taken losses in his career, and he yo-yoed around the top five in high school. But he&a;#39;s got the mat game, he&a;#39;s hard to score on, and from that he can put guys on their back with his straight leg cradle. I&a;#39;ve seen him win Fargo, Super 32, Ironman, FloNationals, PA states, Who&a;#39;s #1, and the U.S. Open. He&a;#39;s at a power program where guys have won before, and he&a;#39;s got the pedigree from one of the most historic areas for wrestling in the country.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;A coaching staff that battles is the one in North Carolina. They fight for rankings, they fight for seeds, they want their recruits mentioned all the time, and they&a;#39;re hungry to become a contender. That&a;#39;s the environment O&a;#39;Connor is in right now, so I don&a;#39;t have much issue seeing him claw through Thursday and Friday in Minneapolis to get to the finals.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Mauller&a;#39;s not fooling me. I frequently give Missouri guys too little credit, not this year. Maybe I&a;#39;m sleeping on him by not picking him to win or make the finals, but he&a;#39;s got top four written all over him this year. He&a;#39;s battled with Lee a ton, mostly in freestyle, and Mauller won the last time they faced off so that&a;#39;s why Brayton goes fourth. I&a;#39;ve also got a tad more faith in Mauller&a;#39;s folk, for now.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Degen and Alirez could see each other as many as four times this year, and so I&a;#39;m going with the prior AA there, but Boo is going to be difficult to keep out of the top five, let alone off the podium at all. Thomsen is 3-0 against Lugo going back to high school, so I&a;#39;m going with history there.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;If you tell me I&a;#39;m getting 2018 Boo Lewallen, guaranteed a full year of health, I&a;#39;d pick him to place. But his body worries me, so he&a;#39;s not in my top eight. Here&a;#39;s hoping for a clean bill of health for all these -16T15:23:33+00:00&q;},&q;enable_interstitial_ad&q;:false,&q;enable_pre_roll_ads&q;:true,&q;shareable_link&q;:null,&q;created_at&q;:&q;2014-10-14T09:04:40+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:&q;2017-11-16T15:23:33+00:00&q;,&q;name&q;:&q;Ryan Type&q;,&q;display_name&q;:null,&q;parent&q;:null,&q;created_at&q;:&q;2018-01-22T21:12:47+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:&q;2019-08-20T20:27:55+00:00&q;},&q;created_at&q;:&q;2018-01-22T21:14:10+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:null},{&q;id&q;:67,&q;name&q;:&q;College&q;,&q;display_name&q;:null,&q;parent&q;:{&q;id&q;:39285,&q;name&q;:&q;Level&q;,&q;display_name&q;:null,&q;parent&q;:null,&q;created_at&q;:&q;2019-09-17T23:44:22+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:&q;2019-09-18T15:34:38+00:00&q;},&q;created_at&q;:&q;2018-02-02T15:06:13+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:null},{&q;id&q;:39160,&q;name&q;:&q;Men&q;,&q;display_name&q;:null,&q;parent&q;:{&q;id&q;:39230,&q;name&q;:&q;Gender&q;,&q;display_name&q;:null,&q;parent&q;:null,&q;created_at&q;:&q;2019-09-17T21:51:26+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:null},&q;created_at&q;:&q;2019-09-17T19:41:42+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:null},{&q;id&q;:40459,&q;name&q;:&q;NCAA&q;,&q;display_name&q;:null,&q;parent&q;:{&q;id&q;:38912,&q;name&q;:&q;Governing Body&q;,&q;display_name&q;:null,&q;parent&q;:null,&q;created_at&q;:&q;2019-08-13T16:19:32+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:&q;2019-09-18T15:34:23+00:00&q;},&q;created_at&q;:&q;2019-10-22T16:53:23+00:00&q;,&q;modified_at&q;:null}],&q;mobile_link&q;:&q;https://admin.flowrestling.org/mobile/articles/6586398-2019-20-ncaa-preview-predictions-157-pounds&q;,&q;preview_text&q;:&q;&l;p&g;The most merciful thing in the world is the inability of the human mind to correlate all its contents. We live on a placid island of ignorance in the midst of black seas of infinity, and it was not meant that we should voyage far. The sciences, each straining in its own direction, have hitherto harmed us little; but some day the piecing together of dissociated knowledge will open up such terrifying vistas of reality, and of our frightful position therein, that we shall either go mad from the revelation or flee from the light into the peace and safety of a new dark age.&l;/p&g;&q;,&q;content&q;:&q;&l;p&g;The most merciful thing in the world is the inability of the human mind to correlate all its contents. We live on a placid island of ignorance in the midst of black seas of infinity, and it was not meant that we should voyage far. The sciences, each straining in its own direction, have hitherto harmed us little; but some day the piecing together of dissociated knowledge will open up such terrifying vistas of reality, and of our frightful position therein, that we shall either go mad from the revelation or flee from the light into the peace and safety of a new dark age.&l;/p&g;&l;p style=\&q;text-align: center;\&q;&g;&l;em&g;Previous Previews: &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/articles/6578344-2019-20-ncaa-preview-predictions-125-pounds\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;125 pounds&l;/a&g; |&a;nbsp;&l;/em&g;&l;em&g;&l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/articles/6581952-2019-20-ncaa-preview-predictions-133-pounds\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;133 Pounds&l;/a&g; |&a;nbsp;&l;/em&g;&l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/articles/6585983-2019-20-ncaa-preview-predictions-141-pounds\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;&l;em&g;141 Pounds&l;/em&g;&l;/a&g;&l;em&g;&a;nbsp;|&a;nbsp;&l;/em&g;&l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/articles/6586184-2019-20-ncaa-preview-predictions-149-pounds\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;&l;em&g;149 Pounds&l;/em&g;&l;/a&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;But we&a;#39;re not going to worry about the consequences of our scientific voyage of discovery. We&a;#39;re going to plunge headfirst in the sea of 157-pound NCAA knowledge, without regard to the terrifying consequences. &a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Speaking of terrifying, Jason Nolf is no longer around to brutalize the 157-pound field. Not counting an injury default, Nolf won an astounding 84 matches in a row, going undefeated in his last three years of eligibility.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;So no Nolf, but a cavalcade of talented contenders return for another season of middleweight mayhem.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;Contenders&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#1 Hayden Hidlay, North Carolina State&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#2 Kaleb Young, Iowa&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#3 Ryan Deakin, Northwestern&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Last year&a;#39;s top three all graduate (#1 Nolf, #2 Tyler Berger, and #3 Alec Pantaleo), which leaves Hayden Hidlay as the preseason favorite. Hidlay won the first 26 matches of his varsity career, taking his first loss and only loss of the 2017-18 season in the 2018 NCAA finals.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Hidlay started last season ranked #2 but an early-season loss to Larry Early knocked down his ranking and was partly to blame for him receiving the #5 seed, which meant Hidlay had to go through Nolf in the semifinals. That infamous match, which you can watch below, ultimately went Nolf&a;#39;s way. Hidlay won his next wrestleback match against Deakin but then fell to Pantelo to finish fourth in his sophomore campaign.&l;/p&g;&l;div class=\&q;froala-video\&q;&g;&l;span class=\&q;fr-video fr-fvc fr-dvi fr-draggable\&q; contenteditable=\&q;false\&q;&g;&l;iframe width=\&q;640\&q; height=\&q;360\&q; class=\&q;flo-video-embed embed embed-responsive-item fr-draggable\&q; src=\&q;/embed/V4paOAbPl\&q;&g;&l;/iframe&g;&l;/span&g;&l;/div&g;&l;p&g;&l;br&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Young and Deakin are 2A and 2B on the contenders&a;#39; list. They met three times last season. Deakin won in the finals of the 56th Midlands, but Young would get his revenge twice in Pittsburgh, winning in sudden victory in both the quarterfinals and the fifth-place match.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Hidlay has the early Vegas odds in his favor, but Young and Deakin aren&a;#39;t far behind. Young will have the pressure of the Hawkeyes&a;#39; potential title run propelling him forward and Deakin will be looking to build off his &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/results/6518440-2019-final-x-lincoln/26662\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;Final X&l;/a&g; appearance over the summer.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;Other Contenders&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#4 Larry Early, Old Dominion&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#5 Brady Berge, Penn State&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#11 Jacori Teemer, Arizona State&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#18 David Carr, Iowa State&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Larry Early&a;#39;s aforementioned victory over the preseason favorite provides evidence for his inclusion in the top tier of contenders. Eight losses on the season though drops the All-American to tier #2. Still, the senior Monarch will be expected to improve on his eighth-place finish from 2019.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Brady Berge didn&a;#39;t make the podium down at 149 in his redshirt freshman campaign. The Minnesota native might be a better fit up at 157 however, and the junior freestyle junior world bronze medalist also has the benefit of another year in one of the best wrestling rooms in the country.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Jacori Teemer and David Carr are two redshirt freshmen who we think have the potential to contend for a title. Teemer finished fifth at Midlands (down at 149) and then won the National Collegiate Open at 157. Carr only dropped one match last season while redshirted, then went to Estonia and won a freestyle junior world championship at 74kg. For those reasons, we are very bullish on these two youngsters.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;em&g;Watch Carr drill live at practice over the summer:&l;/em&g;&l;/p&g;&l;div class=\&q;froala-video fr-draggable\&q;&g;&l;span class=\&q;fr-video fr-fvc fr-dvi fr-draggable\&q; contenteditable=\&q;false\&q;&g;&l;iframe width=\&q;640\&q; height=\&q;360\&q; class=\&q;flo-video-embed embed embed-responsive-item fr-draggable\&q; src=\&q;/embed/zOpoY8lq9\&q;&g;&l;/iframe&g;&l;/span&g;&l;/div&g;&l;p&g;&l;br&g;&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;The Rest of the Best of the Rest&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;You&a;#39;ve seen the best of the rest, now check out the rest of the best.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#6 Eric Barone, Illinois&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#7 Josh Humphreys, Lehigh&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#8 Anthony Artalona, Penn&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#9 Talen Rahmani, Pitt&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#10 Jarrett Jacques, Missouri&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Barone has a win over Young. Humphreys has beaten nearly a dozen national qualifiers including All-American Christian Pagdilao. Artalona made the bloodround as a true freshman. Rahmani also made the bloodround, though at his third NCAA tournament. Finally, Jacques had a very promising true freshman season for the Tigers.&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;Sneaky Blinders &a;amp; Landmines&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;Nomad calls them &a;quot;sleepers,&a;quot; CP and Bratke call them &a;quot;sneakers,&a;quot; we call them &a;quot;sneaky blinders&a;quot; because it sounds like the show Peaky Blinders and that show rules and everyone should watch it.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#15 Logan Parks, Central Michigan&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#21 Quincy Monday, Princeton&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#23 Luke Weiland, Army&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Parks placed seventh at Midlands and went 1-2 at NCAAs. Logan has had close matches with some of the best in the division. He also has a very rad wolf-moon tattoo, which is worth some sleeper points.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;As the son of an Olympic gold medalist, Quincy Monday certainly has the right wrestling pedigree. He also had a solid true freshman season, placing third at EIWAs and qualifying for the NCAAs.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;em&g;Watch Monday pack NCAA qualifier Hunter Ladnier in under two minutes:&l;/em&g;&l;/p&g;&l;div class=\&q;froala-video fr-draggable\&q;&g;&l;span class=\&q;fr-video fr-fvc fr-dvi fr-draggable\&q; contenteditable=\&q;false\&q;&g;&l;iframe width=\&q;640\&q; height=\&q;360\&q; class=\&q;flo-video-embed embed embed-responsive-item fr-draggable\&q; src=\&q;/embed/PBzWvkO8m\&q;&g;&l;/iframe&g;&l;/span&g;&l;/div&g;&l;p&g;&l;br&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Weiland is one of Army&a;#39;s six returning NCAA qualifiers on the Black Knight roster. The senior from Missouri also made a trip to nationals as a sophomore. Weiland, like most of his West Point teammates, is a tough out you do not want to see in your side of the bracket.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;New Blood&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#11 Jacoori Teemer, Arizona State&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#18 David Carr, Iowa State&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;#22 Will Lewan, Michigan&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Justin McCoy, Virginia&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Peyton Robb, Nebraska&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Jaden Mattox, Ohio State&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Mason Phillips, North Carolina&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Kendall Coleman, Purdue&l;/strong&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;157 is a very young weight class, with only eight seniors currently ranked in the top 25 (compared to 15 seniors at 149 and 13 at 165). It&a;#39;s also tied with 125 in having the most ranked freshmen at three. Expect that number to increase as the season progresses.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Besides Teemer and Carr, who we&a;#39;ve already covered, Michigan&a;#39;s Will Lewan snuck into the rankings after a quality true freshman campaign on a redshirt. Other blue blood programs feature blue-chip talent in their first varsity season. McCoy, Robb, Mattox, Phillips, and Coleman all have the potential to earn a ranking, if not an All-American medal.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;Key Dates&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Friday, November 15:&l;/strong&g; Old Dominion at NC State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Will Larry Early be able to replicate his early-season upset over Hayden Hidaly from 2018?&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Friday, November 22:&l;/strong&g; Penn State at Arizona State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;A very tough early test for both Jacori Teemer and Brady Berge. It doesn&a;#39;t get much easier for Berge next week, either, when he is scheduled to wrestle Lehigh&a;#39;s Josh Humphreys in Bethlehem.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Sunday, November 24:&l;/strong&g; Iowa at Iowa State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;How real of a deal is David Carr? We&a;#39;ll find out when he wrestles All-American Kaleb Young in Ames.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Friday-Saturday, December 6-7&l;/strong&g;: Cliff Keen Las Vegas Invitational&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;This tourney should appear as a key date in every weight class preview, as this event is a veritable beast, likely to feature more All-Americans than any other tournaments save the NCAAs and maybe Big Tens. Slated to participate in Vegas are #1 Hidlay, #3 Deakin, #11 Teemer, #13 Thomas, #17 LaPrade, #18 Carr, #22 Lewan, and unranked freshmen McCoy, Mattox, and Robb.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Monday-Tuesday, December 30-31:&l;/strong&g; The 57th Midlands Championships&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Another rematch of Deakin vs Young in the finals perhaps? Throw in Barone, Artalona, Hartman, and many more and you&a;#39;ve got a killer bracket for the prestigious annual midwest tournament.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Friday, January 31:&a;nbsp;&l;/strong&g;Penn State at Iowa&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Young vs Berge but just, in general, this dual meet is going to be out-of-control bonkers entertainment.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;&l;strong&g;Thursday, February 20:&l;/strong&g; Lehigh at Penn&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;EIWA and NCAA seeding will be on the line when Artalona and Humphreys are scheduled to clash.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;Spey&a;#39;s Spredictions&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;1) Hayden Hidlay, NC State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;2) Ryan Deakin, Northwestern&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;3) Kaleb Young, Iowa&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;4) David Carr, Iowa State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;5) Brady Berge, Penn State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;6) Anthony Artalona, Penn&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;7) Josh Humphreys, Lehigh&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;8) Jacori Teemer, Arizona State&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;R12) Eric Barone, Illinois&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;R12) Larry Early, Old Dominion&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;R12) Zach Hartman, Bucknell&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;R12) Jarrett Jacques, Missouri&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;Unlike the coward Daniel Nomad, we predict bloodrounders in these previews.&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;There is a lot of talent at 157, as is usually the case with middleweights. This year the second and third tiers seem unusually deep, at least at the preseason mark, so we expect to be proven wrong many times in our predictions.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;At the top, we think it&a;#39;s finally Hidlay&a;#39;s year to climb to the top of the podium, giving Wolfpack head coach Pat Popolizio his third national champion (after Gwiazdowski and Macchiavello). We think Deakin inches past Young in the semifinals, although it could just as easily go the other way, as it did twice in Pittsburgh.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;We&a;#39;re pretty bullish on junior world champ David Carr, so much so that fourth feels too low. Carr may very well win the whole dang thing and it would not surprise us in the slightest.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;The bottom half is all freshmen or sophomores, who we think will conspire to deny seniors Barone and Early an All-American medal in their final attempt. Harman and Jacques round out the top 12 with more youth.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;We hope you&a;#39;ve enjoyed correlating all the disassociated knowledge regarding the 157-pound weight class this impending NCAA season. In any event, we at least didn&a;#39;t find the new vistas of reality we opened up to be too terrifying. Nomad tackles 165 on Monday. wrestle-off szn, and we&s;re lucky enough to have the #3 Ohio State Buckeyes do theirs live tonight on FloWrestling!&l;/p&g;&q;,&q;content&q;:&q;&l;p&g;It&a;#39;s wrestle-off szn, and we&a;#39;re lucky enough to have the #3 Ohio State Buckeyes do theirs live tonight on FloWrestling!&l;/p&g;&l;p style=\&q;text-align: center;\&q;&g;&l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/signup?redirect=%2Flive%2F18571-ohio-state-wrestle-offs-big-ten-wrestling\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;&l;strong&g;Watch Ohio State Wrestle-Offs Live on Flo&l;/strong&g;&l;/a&g;&l;/p&g;&l;p style=\&q;text-align: center;\&q;&g;Tonight at 7:15 PM Eastern&l;/p&g;&l;p&g;For the results of Tuesday&a;#39;s preliminary matchups, we compiled them &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/articles/6586414-2019-ohio-state-wrestle-off-matchups-set\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;&l;strong&g;here&l;/strong&g;&l;/a&g; and Ohio State posted them all over their social media. And in case you need an excuse to catch them live, or perhaps want to convince your significant other to join you in viewing, we&a;#39;ve got six reasons for you to watch the Buckeye wrestle-offs.&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;1) It&a;#39;s College Wrestling Live on Flo!&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;This is a cop-out, but for real guys, there&a;#39;s college wrestling you can watch. We know what you want &a;mdash; you&a;#39;re missing that Division I content. It&a;#39;s nine matches, featuring seven ranked wrestlers and three blue-chip big boarders. It&a;#39;s going to satisfy your itch until Opening Day next Friday, November 1.&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;2) Is Rocky Jordan Legit?&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;The short answer is yes, but we&a;#39;ll get a better answer tonight. He dominated Fritz Schierl to get to the match against Kaleb Romero, and there were some who thought Rocky would have been the starter at 174 last year had he not broken his foot. With no matches since winning his second state title for St. Paris Graham, there is perhaps no one we&a;#39;re more interested in seeing tonight.&a;nbsp;&l;/p&g;&l;blockquote align=\&q;center\&q; class=\&q;twitter-tweet\&q; data-width=\&q;550\&q;&g;&l;p dir=\&q;ltr\&q; lang=\&q;en\&q;&g;174 lbs Preliminary Results&l;a href=\&q;https://twitter.com/hashtag/GoBucks?src=hash&a;ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw\&q;&g;#GoBucks&l;/a&g; &l;a href=\&q;https://t.co/KrGsXpG7c8\&q;&g;pic.twitter.com/KrGsXpG7c8&l;/a&g;&l;/p&g;&a;mdash; Ohio State Wrestling (@wrestlingbucks) &l;a href=\&q;https://twitter.com/wrestlingbucks/status/1186747906621087744?ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw\&q;&g;October 22, 2019&l;/a&g;&l;/blockquote&g;&l;h2&g;3) What&a;#39;s Jordan Decatur Look Like In A 7-Minute College Match?&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;CP made sure we got this one in. This is at the absolute top of his agenda tonight, seeing how true freshman Decatur holds up in a full-length college match against a guy who&a;#39;s been in a D1 room for several years.&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;4) Should Kharchla Go Right Away?&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;Of the three true freshmen who could start for the Buckeyes, &l;a href=\&q;https://www.flowrestling.org/video/6517157-carson-kharchla-after-his-first-week-at-tosu\&q; rel=\&q;noopener noreferrer\&q; target=\&q;_blank\&q;&g;Carson Kharchla&l;/a&g; seems like the only we&a;#39;re still unsure about it. The first question is if he&a;#39;s better than #12 Ethan Smith, which is possible and we&a;#39;ll find out quickly tonight. The second and more important one though is, does he want to wrestle immediately? He was on a slow trajectory to the top in high school and may similarly want a year to develop in a college room. Mike Mal and Bratke did a tech notes on this matchup, which you can watch below.&l;/p&g;&l;div class=\&q;froala-video fr-draggable\&q;&g;&l;span class=\&q;fr-video fr-fvc fr-dvi fr-draggable\&q; contenteditable=\&q;false\&q;&g;&l;iframe width=\&q;640\&q; height=\&q;360\&q; class=\&q;flo-video-embed embed embed-responsive-item fr-draggable\&q; src=\&q;/embed/V4A5qByEV\&q;&g;&l;/iframe&g;&l;/span&g;&l;/div&g;&l;p&g;&l;br&g;&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;5) What To Expect Of Jaden Mattox?&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;It&a;#39;s possible Mattox is the best top wrestler Ohio State has brought in since Logan Stieber. Though obviously not on Stieber&a;#39;s level, Mattox was a two-time Ohio state champ and won FloNationals on high school, largely because of his ability to turn people. That top game was why Willie once rated him as the best 157lb prospect in high school at the time, but he&a;#39;s leveled off a bit since then. He went 13-3 in his redshirt year but will have to not only separate himself from Elijah Cleary, he may also have to outshine Ke-Shawn Hayes to eventually become the start at 157.&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;6) A Peek At The Future Of 141 For The Buckeyes&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;Unfortunately Dylan D&a;#39;Emilio got hurt so we didn&a;#39;t get him against Quinn Kinner, but Kinner will be on display tonight against two-time All-American Luke Pletcher. The redshirt freshman was a two-time state champ in single class New Jersey and finished his career #15 on the Big Board.&l;/p&g;&l;h2&g;7) Ohio State Firepower On Full Display&l;/h2&g;&l;p&g;Yes, it&a;#39;s teammates that we know they&a;#39;re a whole lot better than. But this is kind of the point of why teams make wrestle-offs public, right? To give their fans a taste of what&a;#39;s in store this year? That includes #1 Kollin Moore, #3 Luke Pletcher, #5 Sammy Sasso, and #17 Gavin Hoffman. These guys are going to be on the podium in March, so why not get a good glimpse at them tonight?&l;/p&g;&l;div class=\&q;froala-video fr-draggable\&q;&g;&l;span class=\&q;fr-video fr-fvc fr-dvi
